## 22 Oct 2026 (evening): EVERYTHING ON THE VPS: n8n STARTS IT, CLAUDE RUNS ON THE API KEY (branch `feature/vps-worker`, account A)
Harsh, 22 Oct 2026: "everything will be automated ... i would suggest that the n8n triggers it and same for the claude".
- **`n8n/store-factory/worker.py`** is a service on the VPS (`tools/vps/dtj-worker.service`, setup `tools/vps/setup_worker.sh`,
  steps `tools/vps/README.md`).
  - It takes every Page Design / Add Shop / New Store request within about 30 s.
  - It runs `claude -p --permission-mode dontAsk --settings .claude/vps-settings.json --max-budget-usd $DTJ_CLAUDE_MAX_USD` on
    `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. It refuses to start without the key or the cap (rule 9).
  - Telegram waits: Claude ends with `DTJ_WAIT: <store_job command>`. The worker reruns that command every 5 min (only
    store_job start/draft/copy/apps/installs/approve, never a shell) and resumes the same session when it exits not-3.
  - A run left unanswered is marked failed and the admin chat is told; 3 in a row stop new starts.
  - Reel requests are not taken yet.
- **`.claude/vps-settings.json`** lists what unattended Claude may run (DTJ's tools only). It denies the Shopify CLI itself,
  theme publish, git push/commit, rm, curl, ssh, the vault and `deploy.py`.
- **Linux support:** `n8n_mcp.token()` reads the environment on Linux; `vault.py pull --env-file` writes the worker's owner-only
  env file; `tools/vps/set_key.py` saves a key at a hidden prompt.
- **VERIFIED:** tests (worker 7, store-factory 189 in all, vault 6); `bash -n` on the setup script.
- **NOT VERIFIED:** anything on the VPS (no access from this laptop), and a real headless Claude run.
- **OPEN for Harsh:**
  1. an Anthropic API key and the per-request cap (spend decisions);
  2. run the README steps as `ai` on the VPS;
  3. stop laptop listeners once the worker runs.

## THE BRAND COPY RUNS FROM TELEGRAM ONLY: DTJ STORE BUILDER (21 Oct 2026, branch `feature/copy-from-telegram`, account A)
Harsh: "all this steps must be done in the telegram bot nothing will be kept here", then he chose "A new DTJ Store Builder app".
So the copy no longer uses the laptop's CLI sign-in (a short-lived token that needs a browser approval) or a PowerShell command.
- **DTJ Store Builder** (`services/install-catcher/tools/store_builder.py create`) is a separate app in DTJ's organization.
  - Permissions: read/write products, publications and legal policies only. The 7 block apps keep none.
  - It has no blocks. After install, Shopify shows the install service's `/builder` page.
  - Its secret goes into the Windows variable `DTJ_STORE_BUILDER_CLIENT_SECRET` and its client id into `wrangler.toml`
    (`BUILDER_CLIENT_ID`). `deploy.py` puts the secret up as `ORG_SECRET_DTJ_STORE_BUILDER`.
  - On an organization store the install service gets a fresh key per call (client credentials) and keeps none.
- **Install service `POST /copy`** (`src/copy.js`, signed like `/licence`: the laptop's CONTROL_KEY or the bot's BOT_KEY) has four
  operations:
  - `target`: read only;
  - `plan`: keep the laptop's plan per job in KV; fixed once the copy starts;
  - `step`: up to 8 items, collections first, needs `approved_by`;
  - `status`.

  Only its own four mutations, with every input rebuilt from allowed fields: no product id, a price required, https pictures only.
  An existing handle or a non-empty policy is skipped, it stops after 3 failures in a row, and every step is an audit row with the
  approver.
- **n8n "Store Factory: Brand Copy"** (id `ioaho57x1Ji0fEnb`) loops step, then progress, then a 2 s pause, until finished, then
  Telegram. The bot starts it after "Save the Answer" when the tap is Approve under `ok_copy` ("Copy Approved?" -> "Start Brand
  Copy").
- **`store_job.py copy`:** plans through the install service, asks the Approve tap, then only watches the status. If Store Builder
  is not installed yet, it tells the person once in Telegram and retries every minute.
- **VERIFIED:**
  - tests: install service JS 20 OK (5 new for `/copy`);
  - install service Python 35 OK (deploy secret, store_builder 2);
  - n8n/store-factory 162 OK; page builder 444 OK;
  - n8n: Brand Copy created (unpublished, both credentials attached, the loop wiring read back); the bot's draft got the trigger
    (5 operations).
- **ROLLBACK:** the bot's live version before this: `2ca24237-c6c7-463e-ab96-924109237e8a`.
- **DONE with Harsh's two yeses** (chat, 21 Oct 2026: "yes, create Store Builder — yes, deploy"):
  1. **DTJ Store Builder** was created and released in DTJ's organization (client id `5704872b2f8cb791487ab19c51530cca`, config
     `services/store-builder-app/shopify.app.toml`, secret in the Windows variable only).
  2. **The install service was deployed** (`/health` 200, 9 apps).

  Live read-only checks:
  - `/copy` status of an unknown job gives 404 "no copy plan";
  - `/copy` target on elevate gives "not installed", which is Shopify's own answer to Store Builder's key request, so that path is
    wired.

  Next: Harsh installs Store Builder once on his new dev store (Dev Dashboard > Apps > DTJ Store Builder > Install app), then sends
  the dev store's link to the bot.
- **NOT VERIFIED:** any real copy; Store Builder's install on a dev store; whether
  the client credentials grant gives a key with the app's permissions.

## THE LINK IS THE STORE TO BUILD IN: THE BRAND IS COPIED INTO IT (21 Oct 2026, branch `feature/brand-copy`, account A)
The first live test of "a whole store from one link" (elevate, run 891) showed the plan was the wrong way round. Harsh: "it didnt
asked me the brand link it has to create the shopify store of it just fetching the current store products".
- **His choices:**
  - the store to build in is whatever store he sends;
  - copy products with pictures and prices, collections, logo/colours/fonts and the policy pages;
  - products ACTIVE when the store is behind a password;
  - **one Approve tap per import** (now in CLAUDE.md rule 2).
- **Questions now start with "which brand?":** the brand's own Shopify link, or "This store is the brand". Products are offered
  from the brand's best sellers.
- **`store_job.py copy`:** a read-only plan (`.work/stores/<job>/copy/plan.json`), then one Approve tap that names the counts and
  notes, then the apply command.
- **`services/store-builder/provision/brand_copy.py`** (API 2026-10; the CLI refuses older versions):
  - it reads the brand's /products.json, collections and policy pages, and the target's handles, currency, empty policies and the
    Online Store channel;
  - it creates with `productSet` (all options and variants, pictures keeping the brand's file names, untracked stock), then
    `collectionCreate` and `publishablePublish`, and `shopPolicyUpdate` only for empty policies;
  - it never touches an existing handle, and nothing deletes (allowlist in `provision/gql.py` extended with these four);
  - prices are the brand's numbers (a currency mismatch is noted, never converted);
  - it resumes from `applied.json`, logs every write with the approver, and stops after 3 failures in a row.
- **Fixed after the live test:**
  - `match_products`: an exact name wins, and same-name products take the best seller (PR #24);
  - every stop is told in Telegram;
  - an answer counts only when given after its question (the table kept the first run's taps for the same job).
- **VERIFIED:**
  - tests: n8n/store-factory 155 OK, page builder 444 OK (brand copy 10, store job 24);
  - read-only on real stores: Shopify's 2026-10 schema read through the CLI (every input field used exists);
  - a real plan Pro Effex -> elevate: 17 products (197 pictures, 58 variants), 29 collections, as DRAFT because elevate is open to
    the public;
  - policies were left out, because elevate's CLI sign-in lacks `read_legal_policies`.
- **NOT VERIFIED:**
  - a real import: `apply` has never run, and auto mode may block `--allow-mutations` (then it is Harsh's PowerShell);
  - picture file names after the copy matching the brand's;
  - the product pages read from the brand's store.
- **Before the first real import, the store's CLI sign-in needs the scopes once:**
  `shopify store auth -s <store> --scopes read_products,write_products,read_publications,write_publications,read_legal_policies,write_legal_policies`
- **Open:** the old elevate job state was moved to `.work/stores/elevate-with-harsh.before-brand-copy`; run 891 is continued with
  the new questions.

## A WHOLE STORE FROM ONE LINK (21 Oct 2026, branch `feature/store-from-link`, account A)
Harsh: "i just share the brand store link and it starts designing the store and it asks the specific questions it needs along with
the app installation". His choices: every part by default; the product-page count asked per store; **Approve taps in Telegram** as
the yes (now in CLAUDE.md rule 2); **his collaborator login** instead of a Theme Access password.
- **Flow:** an admin sends the bot a store's own link (no /products/) -> "Start New Store" -> n8n **Store Factory: New Store**
  (id `nvaph1ZhD1mreJHD`, waits up to 7 days for Claude Code) -> a laptop follows skill `dtj-new-store`:
  `n8n/store-factory/store_job.py start` reads the store (`page_builder/store_scan.py`: original .myshopify.com address, live theme,
  products in best-selling order, collections) and asks in Telegram: whose store, the client's name, the theme question when it is
  not Horizon, what to design, which products (options sized to the store), the offer (typed words only). `draft` = Approve tap, then
  `shopify theme duplicate` of the live theme as "DTJ Draft <date>" (unpublished, read back). `apps` = Approve tap, then the exact
  `add_shop.py` command. Then brand intake, header/footer (tap each), home (no tap), collection (tap), product pages (no tap),
  `finish` -> `reply.py`. A button tap or typed reply goes bot -> "Save the Answer" -> n8n table **dtj_bot_answers**
  (id `k4r02PeayiGh60gW`) -> the laptop reads it (`bot_ask.py`). Every tap is logged with the person's name.
- **Store access:** `theme_target.open_access(store)` = the Theme Access password when saved, else the CLI's own login with
  `SHOPIFY_FLAG_NO_INPUT=1` (a missing login fails at once with what to do). All five page writers use it.
- **Found while building:** the builder designs on Horizon only (Dawn: product pages only). Gymreapers runs Impact, so for such a
  store the job asks the person to add Horizon (unpublished) first, or stops.
- **VERIFIED:** tests: n8n/store-factory 147 OK (store job 16, bot 10 new), page builder 434 OK (9 new), install catcher 32 OK.
  Read-only on real stores: the CLI login listed elevate's themes with no password; `store_scan` read elevate (13 products,
  Horizon), Gymreapers (246, Impact) and Pro Effex (18, Horizon; original address 1sq0y0-3c). n8n: New Store created

## /addshop: A CLIENT SHOP FROM TELEGRAM IN ONE STEP (21 Oct 2026, branch `feature/addshop`, account A)
Harsh: "cant we make the installation process a one step process from telegram ... i will provide the shopify domain of the store".
- **How it works now:** an admin sends the bot `/addshop name.myshopify.com Client Name` (also `admin.shopify.com/store/<name>`).
  The bot starts n8n **Store Factory: Add Shop** (id `W7Aiz9aup2OfAkmR`) without waiting and answers "Got it". That workflow pauses
  at "Wait for Claude Code: Add Shop" (3 days). A DTJ laptop's Claude Code picks it up (`listen.py` now watches Page Design AND Add
  Shop by default, oldest first; the reel VPS keeps `--workflow "Reel Factory: New Reel"`) and runs, in the person's terminal:
  `python services/install-catcher/tools/add_shop.py --shop <x>.myshopify.com --label "<Name>" --chat <id> --approved-by "<who> (Telegram /addshop)"`.
  It creates the 7 app copies in DTJ's organization 141167028 (Blocks, Sections, Sections 2, Heroes, Commerce, Shop, Collection),
  writes + links their files (`add_client.py`, now 7 parts), moves each client secret into the Windows user variable
  `DTJ_SECRET_<CLIENT>_<PART>` (never printed), releases them, deploys the install service, and runs a progress bar in the chat.
  Then the ONE manual step: each copy's install link is made only in the Dev Dashboard (Distribution, Custom distribution, Generate
  link; no API, no CLI), recorded with `--link <part>=<url>` (refused unless the link carries that copy's client id). The next run
  sends 7 Install buttons for the STORE OWNER (the skill's 9 Oct finding: a collaborator login gets "installation link is invalid"),
  waits up to 30 min reporting each install, fills `page_builder/clients/<client>.json` (`client_registry.py`) and adds a dtj_stores
  row (active only with `--theme`). Every run writes `.work/add-shop/<client>.answer.json`; `reply.py` (new stage `add_shop`, "done"
  needs installed = 7) answers n8n, which tells the admin. A rerun continues where it stopped; app files already in the repo are
  reused, so a second laptop never makes duplicate apps.
- **Code:** `services/install-catcher/tools/add_shop.py` (+ `test/test_add_shop.py`, 10 tests), `add_client.py`, `client_registry.py`,
  `src/worker.js` (7 parts); n8n `add_shop_request.js`, `add_shop_reply.js`, `add_shop_notify.js`, `add_shop_started.js`,
  `deploy_add_shop.py`, `add_shop.workflow.json`; `bot_read.js` / `bot_route.js` (`/addshop`, admins only, on `/commands`);
  `deploy_telegram_bot.py` (`addshop_ops`: 8th "What to Do" rule -> "Start Add Shop" -> "Add Shop Started" -> Send);
  `setup_n8n.py` (Add Shop in the workflow list, before the bot); skills `dtj-add-shop` ("One command: add_shop.py") and
  `dtj-factory-setup` (listen handles Add Shop requests); `CLAUDE.md` n8n hand-offs line.
- **VERIFIED:** tests: install catcher Python 32 OK + worker JS 15 OK; n8n/store-factory 123 OK. n8n: the Add Shop workflow validated
  and created (unpublished), the bot's /addshop branch added (11 operations); `setup_n8n.py` check: every code node equals the repo,
  credentials attached; only "Add Shop not published" and "bot draft ahead of live" left (published after the merge, see below).
- **ROLLBACK:** the bot's live version before /addshop: `3909c9d4-36ec-4e98-a2f7-d8b587aa1c23` (n8n workflow history > restore).
  Add Shop is new (unpublish it to switch it off).

## PAGE DESIGN PROGRESS BAR IN TELEGRAM (20 Oct 2026, branch `feature/bot-progress-bar`)
Harsh: "in the telegram bot also add the progress bar for page designing". A page request from the bot now gets ONE Telegram message
that the designer's Claude Code session edits at every step (like the brand intake's bar): reading the brief 0-5 %, collecting the
brand's data 5-20, reading the product 20-30, facts / brand / words 30-55, build + design checks 55-85 (round 1-3 of 3), checking the
draft 85-95, then "Done in N min" (the preview link follows as before) or "Stopped after N min: <why>". The split is a set layout, not
a measured timing; the time is real. Each command always exits 0 and never stops the design.
- Code: `page_builder/progress.py` (`PageProgress`, CLI `python -m page_builder.progress page start|step|done|stopped --state
  <client>/progress.json`; the state file holds the chat id and message id, never the token; the token is the laptop's
  `DTJ_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`). `request_page_design.js` adds the commands to the task list only when the brief has a
  `telegram_chat_id` (a client-form brief gets none) and passes `request.telegram_chat_id`. `bot_answer.js`: "A progress bar shows up
  here when they do". Tests: store-builder 410 OK (4 new), n8n 112 OK.
- n8n: the two code nodes copied with `setup_n8n.py --apply` (drafts 20 Oct 13:17 UTC: Page Design f0bddd8f, Telegram Bot 3909c9d4),
  then published under Harsh's standing merge/publish rule. ROLLBACK targets (the live versions before): Page Design
  `327fb288-9013-4c83-9b13-f3ad0479823f`, Telegram Bot `9307e15b-790f-4630-aba9-a93fd2a38b87` (n8n workflow history > restore).
- Not verified yet: a real page request from Telegram end to end (the bar was tested with a stand-in Telegram).

## BRAND INTAKE INTO GOOGLE DRIVE (19 Oct 2026; skill `.claude/skills/dtj-brand-intake`)
Harsh: "a scraper which will scrape all the product images max to only 20 products ... and a file that contains the details of the
brand and brand color palette", then "uploads to the google drive", "no workspace, go with a dedicated dtj gmail and github actions,
use dtjharshsharma@gmail.com".
- **What it does:** `page_builder/brand_intake.py` reads a client's PUBLIC store (pictures and videos of at most 20 products, logo,
  collections, policies, fonts, theme colours, a palette proposal from `page_builder/palette.py`) into `<client>/intake/` with
  `BRAND.md`, `brand-intake.json` (every fact with its source), a draft `brand.json`, `palette.png`, `products.png`. Colours,
  category and price tier are guesses a person confirms. `--drive "DTJ Clients"` uploads it with `page_builder/drive_upload.py`
  (DTJ's own Google app, `drive.file` only) to `DTJ Clients/<brand>/Brand intake <date>`.
- **Three ways to run:** the bot's `/intake <store>` (n8n starts the GitHub workflow, which answers in the chat with the Drive link),
  `gh workflow run brand-intake.yml -f store=...`, or locally (skill).
- **Set up (19 Oct 2026):** Google Cloud project `dtj-brand-intake` on dtjharshsharma@gmail.com (Drive API on; consent screen
  "DTJ Brand Intake", External, In production, scope drive.file only, no logo; publishing needed a home page and privacy link, so it
  points at digitaltusharjoshi.com and its /privacy-policy/); Desktop client "DTJ Brand Intake laptop"; `--authorize` done on
  Harsh's laptop (keys `DTJ_GDRIVE_*` in the vault and in the repo's Actions secrets with `DTJ_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`:
  `tools/team-setup/github_secrets.py` says "all set"). First real upload from the laptop: Free Soul, 151 files / 45 MB, 7 min 11 s.
- **Not live yet (each one Harsh's yes):** the bot's GitHub key `DTJ_GITHUB_ACTIONS_TOKEN` (fine-grained, Actions read/write on this
  repo, kept in the vault; `setup_n8n.py --apply` makes the n8n Bearer Auth credential "DTJ GitHub Actions"), a first GitHub run,
  `deploy_telegram_bot.py` (adds /intake to the bot's draft), a real `/intake` test, publishing the bot.
- **Not verified yet:** a GitHub Actions run, `/intake` in real Telegram, n8n's real Bearer Auth form.

## SEO SKILL `dtj-seo` (new 19 Oct 2026; `.claude/skills/dtj-seo/`, NOT committed yet)

Harsh asked for an SEO skill (DTJ's own site; jobs: check and explain, approve and apply fixes, keyword research, write a page,
blog pictures with ChatGPT image 2.5; "if the OpenAI API isn't provided, Claude generates using ChatGPT in its own browser";
"top-notch, highly converting, high traffic").
- **What it is:** a runbook over `services/seo-engine` with a 9-point quality bar for every page (real demand, one page per
  question, beats Google's top 3, answer first, earns the click, audit link mid-page and at the end, posts that should link in).
  Scripts: `keyword_check.py` (engine filters on DataForSEO/OpenSEO rows), `page_brief.py` (writer brief + overlap warnings),
  `page_gate.py` (engine gate, HTML, preview, images.md, `--topic-id`, optional WP draft), `blog_image.py` (GPT Image 2.5
  `gpt-image-2.5-flare`/`-sunburst` via `OPENAI_API_KEY`, or `--via browser` for ChatGPT in the built-in browser, attended only;
  refuses numbers, dashboards, logos, real people, text in pictures).
- **Tested** on 3 requests, 2 rounds (`.work/skill-evals/dtj-seo-workspace/`): round 2 with skill 95% of checks, without 91%;
  the article test 11/11 vs 8/11. No live change, no paid call in any test. Pictures have only been dry-run (no key yet).
- **Found while testing:** the engine was never in git (now in PR harsh-shopify-dev/dtj-agency-automation#2 with the sitemap
  retry fix, not merged); the bridge plugin isn't on the live site; `/elementor-hf/*` pages redirect to the home page, so
  apply_batch's live check will say "not visible yet" for c001/c002; the bridge plugin's fallback lookup by the last URL part
  could match the wrong post (test on staging); `facts.json` lists office address as unknown while c004 publishes it from
  `local_proof.json`; engine paragraphs can't hold in-sentence links.
- **Open:** commit the skill (branch + PR), `OPENAI_API_KEY` in Bitwarden and GitHub secrets, Search Console access, merge PR #2.

## SEO / AEO ENGINE FOR DTJ'S OWN SITE (new 14 Oct 2026; full plan in `docs/seo-aeo/README.md`)

Harsh: "create an AI Automation that will do the SEO and AEO of DTJ". His decisions: the Claude **API key** writes, unattended,
weekly; articles go into WordPress as **unpublished drafts** only.

**Built** (uncommitted, on the current branch: Harsh has not asked for a commit):
- **The service** `services/seo-engine/`:
  - `site_scan` (free sitemap and page check);
  - `aeo_probe` (asks Claude with web search and records by code whether DTJ is named or cited);
  - `writer` (one article a week, Claude Opus 5.5);
  - `gates` (no number outside `data/facts.json`, no best/#1, guarantees, Tushar call, COD/RTO promise, process talk or links off the site);
  - `wordpress` (draft only; refuses a slug that exists);
  - `run_weekly`, `report`, `actionlog`.
- **Tests:** 30, all OK.
- **The schedule:** `.github/workflows/seo-weekly.yml`, Mondays 09:00 IST. It runs only from the default branch `chore/initial-import`. Without `SEO_LIVE=true` it only scans.
- **Mode: shadow.** `SEO_MODE=approve` plus the kill switch on and dry run off puts drafts into WordPress.

**Strategy agreed in chat:**
- Harsh's place ladder, local (Rajkot head office, Ahmedabad) → Gujarat → India, for Maps and city searches.
- Alongside it, problem-answer articles across India from week 1.
- Australia and Canada offices left out.

**Verified:** the free scan ran on the live site (24 pages, 3 to fix now). Findings are in the README.

**Not run:** the AI steps (no API key yet) and WordPress (no credentials).

**Open:** API key, WP Editor application password, Search Console access, OpenSEO credits (201 left, no DTJ project), Business
Profiles for Rajkot and Ahmedabad, which clients are in which city, a Telegram message for the report.

**Upgrade, later 14 Oct 2026** (Jono Catliff framework; Harsh: "b first month, DataForSEO, plain founder English, build it").

New in `services/seo-engine`:
- `dataforseo` (keywords, Google top 10, ChatGPT/Perplexity/Gemini answers, spending cap `DFS_MAX_USD`);
- `keywords` (monthly plan: filters and grouping by shared Google results);
- `serp_skeleton` (outline of the top 3 results, plus the copy check);
- `city_page` (service × city, only with proof in `data/local_proof.json`, doorway check);
- `fixer` (weekly fixes batch);
- `apply_batch` (Tier 2, named approver, live check up to 3 rounds, undo);
- `wordpress-plugin/dtj-seo-bridge.php` (only Yoast title, description, noindex and site JSON-LD);
- `.github/workflows/seo-apply.yml`.

**Tests:** 52 OK. The free run on the live site worked: batch `fix-2026-10-14` has 4 ready changes and 6 for a person.

**Not verified:** anything with keys (Claude, DataForSEO, WordPress) and the PHP plugin (no PHP here; test on staging first).

**Details:** `docs/seo-aeo/README.md`.

**UPDATE 20 Oct 2026 (latest: DTJ Product List cart fix, RELEASED as dtj-collection-3 at 10:25 and CONFIRMED by Harsh on the draft theme: cart icon/drawer update without a reload):** Harsh reported Add to cart did not update the theme's cart icon/drawer until a reload. Cause: the elevate draft (newer Horizon) listens for Shopify's standard `shopify:cart:lines-update` event (module `@shopify/events`), which the block never sent. Fix in `src/product-list.js`: the card announces `CartLinesUpdateEvent` (action add, the variant) before `/cart/add.js`, then settles the event's promise with the real `/cart.js` cart; failure falls back to the normal form post. `data-product-id` added to the card. Script 9,920 bytes (limit 10,000). Verified locally only (real Shopify events module in the browser harness, stubbed cart endpoints): behaviour 33/33, style compare 41 of 42 exact, 25 collection tests OK, library check ok (27.1 KB). Release done (was: Harsh must release a new version: `shopify app deploy --path "services\block-library-collection" --allow-updates --message "DTJ Product List: tell the theme's cart when a product is added"`, then reload the theme/preview and I check the cart on the draft.

**UPDATE 19 Oct 2026 (later): BRAND INTAKE for redesigns** (Harsh: "a scraper which will scrape all the product images max to only 20 products ... and a file that contains the details of the brand and brand color palette").
- `services/store-builder/page_builder/brand_intake.py`: `python -m page_builder.brand_intake <store address> --client ../../.work/clients/<client>`. Public pages only, polite (0.25 s between requests), no personal data. Writes `<client>/intake/`: every picture of at most 20 products (hard cap, also when `--max-products` asks for more; the rest only counted), logo, share picture, collection pictures, `brand-intake.json` (facts with sources), `BRAND.md` (readable), a DRAFT `brand.json` (brand-profile schema; category, price tier and colours are guesses listed in BRAND.md), `palette.png`, `products.png`. Colours: candidates from the theme's CSS variables, the logo and the first pack picture of each product; brand colour = theme button > logo > packs; accent = logo > packs > other theme colours (a sale-badge red never wins), 40+ degrees away, no dirt colour for cleaning; then palette.py roles. Non-Shopify sites: home page, logo and colours only.
- v2 after real runs (test store, then freesoul.com as Harsh suggested): videos (each product's videos via `/products/<handle>.js` at ~720p, home-page videos, 400 MB budget, YouTube/Vimeo listed; Harsh: "it should also download videos if available"); dark brand colours count (Freesoul's maroon logo #39000B); a pack colour needs 2+ products; SVG logos read; colours and fonts from a custom theme's stylesheet; status colours (error, stock, sale) ignored; the policy template check reads only the policy wording (an app's "{{" flagged 3 Freesoul policies); 20 s timeout + one retry; re-runs reuse downloaded files.
- Tests `tests/test_brand_intake.py` (10, a stand-in store). Skills: the intake is the first step of a redesign in `dtj-home-page` (H1) and `dtj-pdp-factory` (P2).

## MAIN TASK: READ THIS FIRST (Harsh, 9 Oct 2026; every Claude session must follow it before anything else in this file)

**The goal of this whole project is a STORE FACTORY, in two parts. Everything else is support.**

1. **AI builds the client's store pages with OUR blocks.** Once our apps are installed on a client's store, an automation that uses the **Claude API (with an API key)** takes the client's brand and product facts and designs the pages from our blocks and sections: it **adapts to the client's brand** (colour palette, fonts, shape language, tone) and follows **high-converting conversion-agency structure** (the kind of product pages agencies such as ConversionWise build), instead of one fixed template. The hand-built TOILOVA page is the quality bar and the first example; the automation must reproduce that judgment every time, for any brand. A person approves before anything reaches a store.
2. **The licence kill switch, fully automated.** A hosted service holds the client secrets and runs the commands; Harsh controls it from Telegram (or another channel) with **no terminal and no secret ever typed**.

**What is NOT the main task:** n8n workflows for alerts, heartbeats, lead-audit intake, reports, ad guard or billing. They are supporting parts and are built only when they serve part 1 or part 2. A previous desktop session drifted into them (its `docs/automation-plan.md` ranks audit-to-pitch ahead of page assembly): that ordering is **superseded** by this section.

**Where it stands (9 Oct 2026)**
- Part 1: `services/store-builder/page_builder/` (new, 37 tests) holds the design JUDGMENT as code: `direction.py` (brand -> palette roles with checked contrast, shape language, type treatment, density, motion, one emphasised section per category, each choice with its reason), `recipes/pdp.json` (conversion-minded section order per category), `sections.py` (facts -> block settings; missing facts OMIT a section, nothing is invented), `rules.py` (lint rules L01-L12: contrast, gutters, rhythm, band alternation, one dark band, reviews under the buy buttons, provenance of every text, traceable numbers, demo gating, accent budget), `copy_contract.py` + `prompts/system.md` (the ONLY job a Claude step has: rewrite structural signposts, validated by code), `catalog.py` (checks every setting against the real block definitions), `examples/` (TOILOVA real facts + four synthetic brands that prove it adapts). Not built yet: the Claude API call itself, a screenshot review loop, writing the file to a theme (a Tier 2 push a person approves), per-client app registry, a home-page recipe.

  **Live client store test (9 Oct 2026):** Harsh wants to test on a live client store (full collaborator access) with DUPLICATED apps. Verified in shopify.dev: the client credentials grant (our `licence.py`) "only works when the app and the store belong to the same Shopify organization ... including a client's store", so collaborator access does NOT let us switch the licence on. Needed: an **install catcher** (authorization code grant, expiring offline tokens: 1 h access + 90-day refresh, `expiring=1`; our copies need NO scopes). Design for approval: **`docs/install-catcher-design.md`** (recommends a Cloudflare Worker over n8n, because n8n code steps cannot use crypto or read credentials without server settings: `NODE_FUNCTION_ALLOW_BUILTIN` is empty by default). `shopify app config link --client-id <id> --file-name shopify.app.<client>.toml --no-input` links a NEW app non-interactively, but creating the app itself is a Dev Dashboard click (Harsh). Order matters: the catcher must exist and the copies' app address must point at it BEFORE the client installs. Waiting on Harsh: approve the design (option B/A/C), the client store's address, 3 new empty apps' Client IDs.
  **APPROVED option B and BUILT (9 Oct 2026, not deployed): `services/install-catcher/`** = `src/worker.js` (Cloudflare Worker: `/start/<app>` checks hmac + store pinning + timestamp, redirects to Shopify's authorize page with a signed single-use state; `/callback/<app>` exchanges the code with `expiring=1`, stores the refresh token AES-GCM encrypted in KV bound to app+store, sets `dtj.licence=active` via `metafieldsSet` on `currentAppInstallation` and reads it back; `/webhooks/<app>` app/uninstalled deletes the key; `/licence` = the kill switch: HMAC-signed by `CONTROL_KEY`, fresh timestamp, `approved_by` required, refreshes and stores the NEW pair, writes, reads back, audit row; weekly cron refresh with an optional alert URL; retries 429/5xx max 3; the only mutation allowed is the licence one; no scopes), `test/worker.test.js` (11 tests, stand-in Shopify, 9 safety mutations all caught), `tools/add_client.py` (writes `shopify.app.<client>.toml` for the 3 copies: not embedded, no scopes, app URL /start, redirect /callback, app/uninstalled webhook; registers APPS in `wrangler.toml`), `tools/deploy.py` (KV once, secrets via wrangler stdin from Windows variables, generates + backs up `DTJ_CATCHER_VAULT_KEY` / `DTJ_CATCHER_CONTROL_KEY`, refuses to replace an existing vault key without its backup, deploys, checks /health), `test/test_tools.py` (8 tests, 2 mutations caught), README with the per-client steps. Harsh was given the app setup steps (3 empty Dev Dashboard apps; secrets saved as `DTJ_SECRET_<CLIENT>_BLOCKS/_SECTIONS/_SECTIONS2`; Cloudflare account + workers.dev subdomain + API token `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` + Account ID). ## A. ACCOUNT B MISSION: analyse the whole project, plan the full AI automation and the automated kill switch (added 8 Oct 2026, READ BEFORE SECTION 0)

*(Numbering inside section A is A.0 to A.12: a reference such as "section 9" or "1.4" written inside A means A.9 or A.1.4. References to RUNBOOK sections say RUNBOOK.)*

Written 8 Oct 2026 by the Claude session ("Account A") that built the block library from the start, for Harsh to hand to Account B.
Account B has no memory of our conversations. Everything it needs is in this HANDOFF file plus the files it points to. **Read this section first, then `CLAUDE.md`, `RUNBOOK.md`, the newest UPDATES below (they are stacked newest first right after section 0.2) and `docs/block-library-design.md`.**
Facts below are what was verified on 8 Oct 2026. Anything marked **[VERIFY]** is unproven and must be checked against Shopify's current docs or a real call before it is relied on.

---

### 0. The ask in one paragraph

Today the licence kill switch works, but a human runs commands in a terminal and types three client secrets by hand (one per app). Harsh wants it **automated**: the client secrets and all commands live in a hosted service, and the switch can be flipped from **Telegram (or another channel) with no terminal and no secret ever typed**, for any store and for all three apps in one action. Beyond the kill switch, Harsh wants Account B to analyse the whole repository and **plan the full AI automation** of the agency's repeatable work (the existing RUNBOOK), using this block library as the first productised asset.

Your first deliverable is a **written plan for Harsh's approval**, not code. Do not build, deploy, flip a flag, or touch a store before Harsh approves the plan and the decisions in section 9.

### 1. What exists today (verified)

#### 1.1 The product
A proprietary Shopify block/section library for Digital Tushar Joshi (DTJ), delivered as **theme app extensions** (app blocks, `target: "section"`), with a **per-store licence flag** that hides every block when set to `inactive`.

| App (Dev Dashboard, org Harsh) | Folder | Client id starts | Blocks | Active release (8 Oct) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DTJ Blocks | `services/block-library` | 5952b6 | 8: Hero Points, Rotating Text, Option Picker, Add-ons, Shoppable Reels, Social Proof Line, Testimonials, **Price** | `dtj-blocks-26` |
| DTJ Sections | `services/block-library-sections` | c10a9d | 8: FAQ, Comparison Table, Stories, What's Inside, Not Inside, Science Stats, Marquee, Testimonials Slider | `dtj-sections-11` (95.8 KB of the 100 KB cap) |
| DTJ Sections 2 | `services/block-library-sections-2` | a25789 | 4: Feature Showcase, Split Story Card, Stat Cards, Measured Results | `dtj-sections-2-6` |

- Three apps exist only because Shopify caps one app at **100 KB of Liquid** and one extension. Each app has its **own client id and client secret** (Shopify issues no master key).
- Source of truth: `manifests/<id>.json` + `src/<id>.liquid/.css/.js`. `services/block-library/tools/build_blocks.py` generates `extensions/<ext>/blocks/dtj-<id>.liquid` and `assets/`. Never edit generated files. Shared tools live only in `services/block-library/tools` (build, rule checker `check_library.py` rules 1-20, `licence.py`, preview renderers).
- Tests: DTJ Blocks 146 (incl. licence tool), DTJ Sections 77, DTJ Sections 2 50. Run with the venv python `D:\CODE CONTAINERR\WEB DEV\dtj-agency-automation\.work\.venv\Scripts\python` (use the ABSOLUTE path: a relative one failed silently twice). Run Sections apps with `DTJ_APP_ROOT=<app folder>`.
- Deploy: `shopify app deploy --path "services/<app>" --allow-updates --message "..." --no-color`, then **always** confirm with `shopify app versions list --path services/<app> --no-color` (the star = active). CLI 4.8.5. Both are slow; use long timeouts.
- Test store (the ONLY store we work in): `elevate-with-harsh.myshopify.com`. Live theme `188778578093` is never touched. Test theme `188893495469` ("DTJ master 4.2.0 test 1", unpublished). Test page: `/products/toilova-1?view=claude-test`, built by a throwaway generator (see 1.4).

#### 1.2 How the kill switch works now
1. Every block starts with `{% render 'dtj-licence', value: app.metafields.dtj.licence.value, ... %}` (generated; see `snippets/dtj-licence.liquid`). The value is an **app-data metafield** `dtj.licence`, stored on the **app installation** (per app, per store). `active` shows the block; anything else, including a fresh install with no value, **shows nothing** (fail closed). In the theme editor an inactive block shows "flag seen: inactive".
2. Writing the flag needs an access token for that store and app. `services/block-library/tools/licence.py` gets one with the **client credentials grant** (client id from `shopify.app.toml`, secret from an environment variable), then reads/sets the metafield with `metafieldsSet` (the only mutation it may send). Every change needs `--approved-by` and appends a row to `.work/out/licence-log.jsonl`.
3. Harsh's terminal flow: `services/block-library/tools/dtj-licence.cmd|.ps1` (`hide`, `show`, `status`, `-App`, `-DryRun`) asks for each app's secret hidden. `licence.py --app all` loops the three apps.
4. Proven on 8 Oct 2026: flag flips work on the test store for all three apps (Harsh ran them).

#### 1.3 Why this is not enough
- **Client credentials only work for stores in OUR Shopify organization.** A paying client's store is outside it. Shopify's docs say client credentials cannot reach a client's store. For client stores we need the **authorization-code (OAuth) install flow** caught by a hosted backend that stores each store's **offline token** in a vault. Without that, "instant revoke" is a promise we cannot keep (we would depend on the client uninstalling).
- **Human toil and risk:** 3 secrets typed by hand; one HTTP 400 loop came from a secret of another app; one secret was exposed in a screenshot on 8 Oct 2026 (**the DTJ Sections client secret `shpss_194d...` must be rotated in the Dev Dashboard**, plus treat any other secret ever pasted into a chat as burnt).
- No monitoring, no billing link, no audit beyond a local jsonl file, no fallback if Harsh is unreachable.

#### 1.3b CORRECTION (9 Oct 2026, verified in shopify.dev docs "About app distribution" and "Select a distribution method"): OUR THREE APPS CANNOT SERVE MANY CLIENT STORES
Harsh was told by another Claude session that "we can't use our apps in other stores". **That is correct for the apps as they exist today**, and earlier text in this repo (the "client stores" paragraph of `docs/block-library-design.md`, and the client-install flow A in section 4 below) assumed otherwise. Facts from Shopify's docs:
- All three apps (DTJ Blocks, DTJ Sections, DTJ Sections 2) were created with **Custom distribution**. A custom app can be installed on **one single store, or several stores of the SAME Plus organization, or development stores** (our own test store is a dev store in our org, which is why it works). It is meant for one merchant.
- **The distribution method cannot be changed after it is chosen.** These three apps can never become public.
- **Public distribution** is the model for many merchants: it needs a Shopify App Store listing and **Shopify's review**. The listing can have **limited visibility** (not searchable; merchants install from its App Store URL). Only public apps can be listed. Theme-app-extension-only apps are the normal shape of a public app, and Shopify requires theme app extensions for App Store apps that touch themes. Public apps get tokens through the authorization code grant (or token exchange if embedded) and must meet App Store requirements (for example privacy/compliance webhooks **[VERIFY the current list]**).
- Consequence for the licence kill switch: the hosted service + token vault in this plan only makes sense for stores that can install our app. Today that means **our own dev stores and at most one merchant per app**.
Options for serving many clients (Harsh must choose, see decision D9): (1) NEW apps with Public distribution and limited-visibility listings (re-publish the same blocks; three apps because of the 100 KB cap; review time and App Store rules apply); (2) one custom app (or set of three) PER client store (every release repeated per client: not scalable); (3) ship the sections as THEME CODE inside each client's theme (portable and no app install, but no Shopify-enforced kill switch; a licence check would have to call our server or use a theme setting); (4) a client that is a Plus organization with several stores can share one custom app. Account B must evaluate these before building P4/P5.
**Harsh's idea, confirmed possible (docs "About stores", 9 Oct 2026): per-client duplicates of our apps installed through COLLABORATOR access.** When a merchant grants our organization collaborator access, their store appears in our Dev Dashboard Stores list as a "Collaboration" store, and the docs table says installing custom apps there "depends on merchant permissions". (Client-transfer stores cannot take custom apps; dev stores can.) So for a client: create 3 NEW custom apps in OUR organization (same code, new client id and secret each), link the same code folders to them with a per-client config file (`shopify app config link`, a `shopify.app.<client>.toml`; **[VERIFY]** the CLI's multi-config workflow), `shopify app deploy` each one, generate the install link for that client's store and have the client (or our collaborator account if the permission allows) install it. Costs: 3 apps and 3 installs per client, every release repeated per client (script it in CI, Telegram-approved), 3 secrets per client for the licence service, and an app-count limit **[VERIFY]**. Nothing built so far is wasted: the blocks, tests, tools and templates are unchanged; only app records, deploy loops and the secret map multiply. Public limited-visibility apps remove the per-client multiplication but need Shopify's review.

### 2. Standing rules Account B must keep (do not relax)

- Work ONLY in `elevate-with-harsh.myshopify.com`. Shopify MCP currently points at `sagarchudesara.com`, a real store of another seller: **never write to it**. Meta Ads MCP has write access to client accounts: do not use.
- Never push to a live theme; narrow pushes `--only <file> --nodelete` to the test theme, pull and diff first, read back after. Never run `shopify auth logout` (signs out every store). Never read the CLI login file `%APPDATA%\shopify-cli-kit-nodejs\Config\config.json`.
- Risk ladder (RUNBOOK 1.1): Tier 0 read-only free; Tier 1 reversible within caps and logged; **Tier 2 (deploy/release an app version, push a theme, change products, flip the licence flag, send a message) needs Harsh's approval of the EXACT action each time**; Tier 3 (delete, force-push, remove access) never. A notification or "it's not showing" is not approval.
- No invented prices, offers, claims, ratings, reviews, scarcity, policies or numbers. Demo content only where Harsh says so, labelled "Demo".
- Secrets never in files, chat, logs, screenshots or workflow JSON. Do not open `.env`. Unattended AI steps use the **Claude API with an API key**, never a subscription login.
- Git: feature branch + PR, never commit to `main`, never force-push, commit only when asked. Update `docs/HANDOFF.md` at the end of every session. Report what was and was not verified.
- Harsh's quality rules: re-verify visually; for a ported original, copy the original exactly; after every deploy confirm the active version; plain founder-friendly language, result first.

### 3. Requirements for the automated kill switch

| # | Requirement |
|---|---|
| R1 | One action hides or shows **all three apps** on one store (and a "panic" action for ALL stores). Per-app control stays available. |
| R2 | Triggered from **Telegram** (primary). A second independent channel (web page with passkey or an n8n form) so one channel's failure cannot lock Harsh out. The terminal script stays as break-glass. |
| R3 | **No secret is ever typed, pasted, shown or logged.** Client secrets and per-store tokens live only in a vault / secret manager. |
| R4 | Works for stores **outside our org** (OAuth install flow, offline token per store and app). |
| R5 | Effect visible on the storefront on the next render; the bot reports the confirmed state read back from Shopify, not just "request sent". |
| R6 | Every action writes an audit row (RUNBOOK 2.5: what, why, evidence, before, after, approver) and is alertable. |
| R7 | Safe by design: allow-listed Telegram user ids, two-step confirm for any change, expiry, rate limits, idempotency, dry-run mode, global `AUTOMATION_ENABLED` flag. |
| R8 | Automatic rules (billing lapse etc.) exist but start in **shadow**; they only become active after Harsh's written durable authorization (decision D1). |
| R9 | Heartbeat and uptime monitoring; a silent failure raises an alert. |
| R10 | Defined behaviour when the service is down (the flag already lives in Shopify, so storefronts keep their last state; see D3). |
| R11 | Everything testable offline with a fake Shopify (the existing `FakeShopify` pattern in `tests/test_licence_tool.py`). |
| R12 | Secret rotation is a routine, documented procedure that needs no downtime. |

### 4. Target architecture

```
 Harsh (Telegram)                     Billing events (Razorpay/Stripe/invoice tool)  [shadow first]
        |  webhook (secret-token header)             |  signed webhook
        v                                            v
 +--------------------------------  Licence service (small Python service; one container)  ---------------------------+
 |  auth: allow-listed user ids      policy: Tier 2 confirm / durable-authorization rules / rate limits / dry-run        |
 |  command router -> action queue -> executor -> read-back verify -> audit log -> Telegram reply + Slack/Telegram alert |
 +-------------+------------------------------------------+---------------------------+-------------------------------+
               |                                          |                           |
      Secret manager (3 client secrets,          Token vault (encrypted DB:      Shopify Admin GraphQL
      bot token, signing keys)                   store x app -> offline token)   metafieldsSet dtj.licence
               ^                                          ^                           ^
               |                                          |                           |
        rotation runbook                    OAuth callback (install) +        app/uninstalled webhook (HMAC) ->
                                            token exchange per client store   mark install removed, alert
```

Components
1. **Licence service** (Python, matches the repo; reuse `licence.py` logic as a library, do not rewrite it). Hosting candidates: Fly.io, Render or Cloud Run (needs always-on HTTPS for webhooks). Decide in D5.
2. **App registry** (config, no secrets): the three apps with handle, client id (from each `shopify.app.toml`), secret reference name, API version pin (currently `2026-07` in `licence.py`).
3. **Token vault**: table `installs(shop, app_key, token_ciphertext, scopes, installed_at, status, last_verified_at)`; encrypt with a key from the secret manager (AES-GCM, key id stored). For own-org test stores the service can mint short-lived tokens with client credentials instead of storing them.
4. **Policy engine**: pure code, no AI. Decides whether an action runs now, needs a confirm tap, or is refused. Reads `AUTOMATION_ENABLED`, per-store flags, caps (e.g. max N changes per hour), protected stores, allow-list.
5. **Executor + verifier**: sets the metafield, then reads it back with a second call and compares. Retries on 429/5xx with backoff (max 3), refresh token once on 401, then stop and alert (RUNBOOK 6).
6. **Audit log**: append-only table (+ daily export to BigQuery/GCS once Phase 0 data layer exists). Every row has `run_id`, actor, store, app, before, after, result, evidence.
7. **Telegram bot**: webhook mode, validates the `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` header, replies in plain language. No secret ever appears in a message.
8. **n8n** (already part of the plan, RUNBOOK 2.1): thin glue only (scheduled heartbeat, billing webhook relay). The kill logic lives in the service, not in n8n workflow JSON.

#### 4.1 Flows
- **A. Install of a client store** (ONLY valid once decision D9 is made: with the current custom-distribution apps this works for dev stores and one merchant per app, see 1.3b): client opens our custom-distribution link, approves; Shopify redirects to our OAuth callback; service verifies `hmac` and `state`, exchanges the code for an offline token, stores it encrypted, sets `dtj.licence` to the **default for new installs (D4: `inactive` until payment is confirmed)**, alerts Harsh. **[VERIFY]** the exact current install/OAuth endpoints, required scopes (the flag write worked with client credentials and no extra scope; confirm what an authorization-code token needs), whether the dev dashboard's "custom distribution" install link supports a redirect to our backend, and offline vs online token behaviour.
- **B. Manual hide/show (Tier 2, human approved)**: Harsh sends `/hide storename`; bot lists exactly what will change ("Hide DTJ Blocks, DTJ Sections, DTJ Sections 2 on elevate-with-harsh.myshopify.com") with Confirm / Cancel buttons (60 s expiry, one-time nonce); on Confirm the executor runs, verifies, replies "Done, read back: inactive x3".
- **C. Automatic rule (billing lapse)**: billing webhook (HMAC-verified, schema-validated, idempotent) says invoice X is N days overdue; policy engine checks the rule set Harsh authorised (D1); in shadow it only messages "I would hide store Y"; in auto mode it acts and notifies. Restoring on payment is the same path in reverse. Hide is client-visible harm if wrong, so auto mode needs a grace period, a reminder ladder and a human veto window.
- **D. Uninstall webhook**: mark the install `removed`, delete the stored token (cannot be reused anyway), alert. No Shopify call is possible afterwards; this is information only.
- **E. Fan-out**: one store action becomes up to three per-app calls with per-app results; partial failure is reported per app and retried, never silently ignored.
- **F. Heartbeat**: the service pings an external uptime monitor every minute and writes a "last_ok" row; a nightly job reads the real flag of every store/app and compares it with the desired state in the database (drift report: someone changed it by hand, or an install is missing).
- **G. Break-glass**: `tools/dtj-licence` stays working for own-org stores with secrets typed by hand; documented as the fallback if Telegram/the service are down.

#### 4.2 Telegram command set (proposal)
`/status [store]` (read-only), `/hide <store> [app]`, `/show <store> [app]`, `/hideall <store>`, `/panic` (hide every store, always asks twice), `/pending` (list approvals), `/mute`, `/help`. Only user ids on the allow-list get a reply; everyone else gets silence and an alert. Group chats are rejected. The bot never accepts a secret: if a message looks like a secret (`shpss_`, long hex) it deletes it if it can and tells Harsh to rotate that secret.
Alternatives weighed: **WhatsApp** (contacts people = Tier 2 template rules, heavier), **Slack** (RUNBOOK's alert channel but not connected here), **web dashboard with passkey** (good second channel), **email magic link** (slow). Recommendation: Telegram + a minimal web page + the CLI break-glass.

### 5. Data model (minimum)
- `apps(app_key, handle, client_id, secret_ref, api_version)`
- `installs(shop, app_key, token_ciphertext, key_id, scopes, status, installed_at, last_verified_at)`
- `desired_state(shop, app_key, state, reason, set_by, set_at, expires_at?)`
- `actions(id, run_id, ts, actor, shop, app_key, action, before, after, result, evidence, approver, error)`
- `approvals(nonce, action_json, requested_by, expires_at, status)`
- `authorizations(rule_id, text, signed_by, signed_at, scope, expires_at)`  (D1: the durable permission, so the system can show "why was this allowed")
Per-client isolation (RUNBOOK 2.4): no query crosses clients; one client's token is never loaded for another's action.

---

### 6. Security design (summary)
- Secrets: a managed secret store (GCP Secret Manager, Doppler or the host's encrypted secrets). Never in git, `.env` committed files, logs, Telegram, screenshots. A repo secret scanner in CI (gitleaks) blocks pushes that contain `shpss_`, `shpat_`, bot tokens.
- Least privilege: the app's own scopes only; no delete scopes; the executor can call **only** `currentAppInstallation` reads and `metafieldsSet` on key `dtj.licence` (allowlist enforced in code, as `licence.py` already does).
- Telegram: webhook secret token, user-id allow-list, confirm buttons with nonce and expiry, per-user rate limit, message size limit, no logging of message bodies beyond the command.
- Shopify webhooks: verify HMAC and timestamp, validate JSON against `schemas/`, idempotency key, dead-letter queue for anything malformed (RUNBOOK 7).
- Rotation: documented procedure per secret (rotate in Dev Dashboard, update secret store, restart, run `/status` all). The leaked DTJ Sections secret is the first rehearsal.
- Fail direction: licence absent or unreadable = block hidden (already so in Liquid). The service being down never changes storefronts by itself.
- Unattended AI: none in the kill path. The switch is deterministic code. AI (Claude API with a key) only assists around it (summaries, anomaly notes) with spend caps.
- Compliance: the service stores store domains and tokens, not shopper data. **[VERIFY]** whether Shopify's mandatory privacy webhooks apply to our distribution type and implement if so.

### 7. Phased plan with exit criteria (propose; Harsh approves each phase)

| Phase | Deliverable | Exit criteria |
|---|---|---|
| **P0 Prerequisites (Harsh + Account B)** | Rotate the exposed secret; create `main` and branch protection on GitHub; push the feature branch and open the PR (Harsh approves the exact commands); pick hosting, secret store, billing source (D5-D7); create the Telegram bot via BotFather (token goes to the secret store only). | PR merged or consciously deferred; secrets in the store; decisions D1-D8 answered in writing. |
| **P1 Service skeleton, dry-run only** | Repo `services/licence-service/` (FastAPI or similar), app registry, config, audit table, `AUTOMATION_ENABLED`, health endpoint, uptime heartbeat, **dry-run mode on every action**, fake-Shopify tests, CI that runs all four test suites + secret scan. | A dummy action runs end to end in dev, writes an audit row, is stopped by the kill flag, and a malformed payload lands in the dead-letter queue (RUNBOOK Phase 0 "done when"). |
| **P2 Own-org stores, parity with the CLI** | Library extracted from `licence.py` (read status, set status, verify), client-credentials token minting with secrets from the secret store, `status`/`set` for all three apps incl. fan-out and read-back verify, drift report. | On `elevate-with-harsh`: service hides/shows all three apps and the storefront page source loses/regains `class="dtj-b` (this also closes the open "flag off" check). |
| **P3 Telegram, approve mode** | Bot webhook, allow-list, `/status`, `/hide`, `/show`, `/hideall`, confirm buttons, audit + alerts, break-glass doc. | Harsh flips the test store from his phone with no terminal; unauthorised user gets no reply and triggers an alert; replay of an old confirm button fails. |
| **P4 Client install flow (OAuth)** (needs D9 first; with today's custom apps only dev stores and one merchant per app qualify) | OAuth callback, token vault, default state on install, uninstall webhook, per-client config, onboarding checklist item "kill switch connected and verified". | A second store (a dev store in our org first, then a consenting client) installs via link, receives a token, can be hidden/shown from Telegram; uninstall is detected. **[VERIFY items from 4.1 resolved in writing.]** |
| **P5 Billing rules in shadow** | Billing webhook relay, rule engine with grace + reminder ladder, shadow messages only. | Two weeks of shadow output reviewed by Harsh with zero wrong "would hide" cases (RUNBOOK 1.2/12 exit criteria). |
| **P6 Observability and runbook** | Dashboards (actions by tier, drift, approvals pending), alert routes, on-call runbook, rotation drill, restore-from-backup drill. | Monthly drill passes; a killed service raises an alert within 2 minutes. |
| **P7 Release automation** | CI/CD for the apps: PR runs tests, rules, Theme Check; a Telegram "release?" button runs `shopify app deploy` with a non-interactive CLI token and then verifies the active version (Tier 2: each release is one approval). **[VERIFY]** the supported non-interactive auth for CLI 4.8.x (`SHOPIFY_CLI_PARTNERS_TOKEN`) and Dev Dashboard apps. | A release is one tap, with the version-list verification posted back. |
| **P8 (optional, later) Auto mode** | Turn the billing rule on for real, only for stores where Harsh signed the authorization (D1). | Needs explicit written approval and the section 12 criteria of the RUNBOOK. |

Order matters: P1 to P3 give Harsh the Telegram kill switch for his own store quickly; P4 is what makes it real for clients; P5 and P8 are policy-heavy and come last.

### 8. The wider "full AI automation" plan Account B should analyse and extend
Account B's second deliverable is to read the whole repo against `RUNBOOK.md` phases and say what to automate next and in what order. Starting hypotheses from the block-library work:
1. **Screenshot-to-block pipeline** (Claude API with an API key, vision): input an image, output manifest + liquid + css + tests; the existing builder, rule checker (20 rules learned from real Shopify refusals), unit tests and render-vs-image comparison are the guardrails; a human reviews the visual diff before any deploy. Tier 0/1 until the deploy step (Tier 2).
2. **Page assembly**: product sheet + brand config -> Horizon template JSON from block references (`gen_toilova_page.py` pattern), validated by the schema-checker, pushed to an unpublished theme only after approval. Ties into RUNBOOK Phase 4 (store build pipeline) and `docs/shopify-provisioning.md`.
3. **QA automation**: headless-browser screenshots at 1280/390/320, overflow stress (9 layouts), Theme Check, storefront-with-flag-off check, speed (indicative only from this PC), report with numbers and their source.
4. **Release automation** (P7 above) and **audit-to-pitch** (RUNBOOK Phase 3) reuse the same approval bot.
5. **Data layer, ad guard, reporting** (RUNBOOK Phases 0, 2, 5): independent of the block library; the Telegram approval bot built here is the shared "approve" surface for them.
Rules for all of it: shadow first, numbers from code, schema-validated AI output, versioned prompts with an eval set, spend caps, "needs human" instead of guessing (RUNBOOK 8).

### 9. Decisions Harsh must make (Account B must ask, not assume)
- **D1 Automatic hiding.** RUNBOOK says Tier 2 never becomes automatic. Hiding a paying client's sections is client-visible. Does Harsh give **durable written authorization** for deterministic rules (e.g. invoice N days overdue after M reminders), per client, with a veto window? If no: the Telegram button is the only trigger (still no terminal, still "no secrets typed"). If yes: RUNBOOK 1.1 must be amended and the authorization stored in `authorizations`.
- **D2 Who may press the button.** Only Harsh, or named staff? Two-person rule for `/panic` and `/hideall`?
- **D3 Service-down behaviour.** The flag lives in Shopify so storefronts keep their last state. Is that acceptable? (Alternative: a signed expiry on the flag so a store hides itself if never renewed; costs complexity.)
- **D4 Default for a new install.** `inactive` until payment confirmed (safest for IP) or `active` for a trial period?
- **D5 Hosting and cost** (Fly/Render/Cloud Run), region, who pays, backups.
- **D6 Billing source** (Razorpay, Stripe, invoicing tool, manual) and webhook availability.
- **D7 Secret store** (GCP Secret Manager, Doppler, host secrets) and who can read it.
- **D9 Distribution model (blocks everything for client stores, see 1.3b):** public limited-visibility apps with App Store review, per-client custom apps, theme-code delivery without an app, or Plus-organization clients only? Also who owns the Partner organization and the App Store listing, and how long a review delay is acceptable.
- **D8 Client contracts and wording**: the licence/kill-switch terms the client agrees to; data retention for install records. (Confirm with a qualified professional, RUNBOOK 11.)

### 10. Testing strategy
- Unit: policy engine, Telegram parsing/confirm/expiry, HMAC verification, allowlist, idempotency, token encryption round trip, executor with `FakeShopify` (token errors, 429, userErrors, drift).
- Contract: replay recorded Shopify responses; a nightly job calls the real read-only `currentAppInstallation` query on the test store.
- Integration (staging): one dev store per app combination; run the full A-G flows; storefront check that page source contains no `class="dtj-b` when hidden and contains it when shown.
- Failure drills: service down, Telegram down, wrong secret (HTTP 400), revoked token, partial fan-out, duplicate webhook, malformed webhook, rotated secret.
- Security: secret scan in CI, dependency audit, a test proving no log line or reply contains a token.

### 11. Risks
| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Wrongly hiding a paying client | confirm taps, grace + reminder ladder, veto window, shadow first, per-client protected flag, read-back verify, instant `/show` |
| Token vault leak | encryption with a separate key, minimal scopes, per-store tokens, rotation drill, alert on unusual reads |
| Telegram account takeover | user-id allow-list, 2FA on Telegram, confirm nonce, `/panic` limited, second channel, audit alerts |
| Service outage | flags live in Shopify, break-glass CLI, heartbeat alert, documented restore |
| Shopify API or install-flow changes | pinned API version, nightly read-only contract test, [VERIFY] list kept current |
| Secrets pasted into chats again | bot refuses and warns; CI secret scan; masked prompts (`-MaskInput`) in the CLI |
| Scope creep into AI-in-the-loop for the switch | the kill path stays deterministic code; AI only advises |

5. Ask Harsh about the Royal Dosa project (A.13) and record the answer.
6. Update this file and open a PR from a feature branch.

Fixes found by that: the space between the two heading parts was lost (now `{{ ' ' }}`); heading and text were too big and heavy against the screenshot (28 px / 14 px / weight 700); bullets that wrap keep the check beside the first line. **A real bug caught by a new test: with 5 stars chosen the card showed 4.** The LOCAL Liquid engine (python-liquid) evaluates `n <= 5` as `n < 5`; Shopify does not, but the check was rewritten as `n < limit` (limit = stars + 1) so both engines agree. No other Liquid source in any of the three apps uses `<=`. LESSON: avoid `<=` in Liquid; use `<` with a limit. Checks: DTJ Sections 2 now 2 blocks, 29.8 KB of 100 KB, 31 tests (8 new for this block), rules ok, overflow OK in 9 layouts, Theme Check 0 errors (1 advisory settings-count warning per big block); DTJ Blocks 130 tests, DTJ Sections 77 tests, both unchanged. **UPDATE 15 (6 Oct 2026): DAWN CHECK (Harsh asked: do the fixes work in other themes?).** Dawn's stylesheets and sections were fetched from the PUBLIC GitHub repo `Shopify/dawn` (raw files, no store touched) into `.work/dawn/` (git-ignored: `base.css`, `section-main-product.css`, `apps.liquid`, `main-product.liquid`; re-fetch with curl from `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Shopify/dawn/main/<path>`). Dawn renders an app block straight into a plain container (`sections/apps.liquid` -> `.page-width`; `main-product.liquid` -> `.product__info-container` via `{% render block %}`), with no centring flex column. `tools/preview_overflow.py` now has 9 layouts (added `dawn-apps`, `dawn-product`, each loading Dawn's real CSS; `THEME_FILES` maps a layout-name prefix to the stylesheets) and an `OVERFLOW_STRIP_FIX=1` CONTROL mode that removes the two shared fixes. Result with the fixes: all 9 DTJ Sections blocks and all 7 DTJ Blocks blocks stay inside in ALL 9 layouts. CONTROL without the fixes: slider and stories STICK OUT in Dawn too (~880-900px, so Dawn needs the long-word `overflow-wrap` fix as well); the marquee overflows only in the Horizon-style wrapper (not in Dawn), so the width-fill wrapper rule matters for Horizon-like themes. So the test is sensitive and both fixes are useful beyond Horizon. NOT tested: any other theme; real browsers older than Chrome 105 / Safari 15.4 / Firefox 121 (they ignore the `:has()` wrapper rule; the blocks still work but that rule does not apply). Before onboarding a client: fetch THEIR theme's CSS and add a layout to `preview_overflow.py`.
**UPDATE 14 (6 Oct 2026): `dtj-sections-4` / `dtj-blocks-22` DID NOT FIX THE OVERFLOW in the real editor (Harsh: "the testimonial for mobile broke even more": block ~720px wide in a 390px viewport, text and arrows cut off, horizontal scrollbar).** My overflow test used GUESSED wrapper layouts and no theme CSS, so it said OK. **Rule from now on: test blocks inside the REAL theme structure with the REAL theme stylesheet.** Real cause, reproduced: Horizon's `base.css` sets `overflow-wrap: break-word` DIRECTLY on `p, h1-h6`, which overrides the value inherited from `.dtj-b`, and `break-word` (unlike `anywhere`) does not shrink min-content, so one long word made the block as wide as the word; the block sits in `.shopify-block` (a flex item of `.layout-panel-flex--column` with `align-items: center|flex-start`), whose width is fit-content, so it grew. Second finding: any block whose content is intrinsically wide (marquee strip, slider row) also makes that wrapper wide even without long words. Fixes, both in the shared BASE_CSS in `tools/build_blocks.py` (so BOTH apps regenerate): `.dtj-b, .dtj-b * { overflow-wrap: anywhere }` (every element, beats the theme's p/h rules) and `.shopify-block:has(> .dtj-b) { width:100%; max-width:100%; min-width:0 }` (the wrapper fills the space; `:has` needs a modern browser). `tools/preview_overflow.py` now has 7 layouts: 3 generic + 2 Horizon-style (wrapper + flex column) + 2 using the theme's real classes/vars WITH `.work/master-packs/assets/base.css` loaded; `preview_block.py` has `PREVIEW_THEME=1` (same real structure + stylesheet). BEFORE the fix: slider stuck out 1,211-2,422 px in the theme layouts (matches the screenshot). AFTER: all 9 section blocks and all 7 DTJ Blocks blocks OK in all 7 layouts; slider rendered on a phone inside the theme wrapper looks right. Tests: app 1 = 127, app 2 = 66, rules ok, Theme Check 0 errors. **DEPLOYED (Harsh approved both): DTJ Blocks `dtj-blocks-23` and DTJ Sections `dtj-sections-5`, both confirmed ACTIVE with `shopify app versions list`.** Note: the Sections deploy printed only "Releasing an app version ..." and ended with NO success box; the version had been created but was INACTIVE (the old `-4` was still live). It was finished with `shopify app release --path "services\block-library-sections" --version dtj-sections-5 --allow-updates` (same approved effect). **ALWAYS confirm a deploy with `shopify app versions list --path <app>` (look for the star = active); do not trust a truncated output, and when running two deploys, run them in SEPARATE commands.** NOT yet verified in the real editor (Harsh's next screenshot).
**UPDATE 13 (6 Oct 2026): HARSH'S COMPLAINT: "the testimonial section design sucks and is not the exact same as my real sections".** Screenshot of `dtj-sections-3` in the real editor showed (1) a design that was my own simplified version, not his real pack section, and (2) the review text running past the card edge with the arrows cut off: the block was WIDER THAN THE PAGE. **Lesson (important): when Harsh says "as it is", port the ORIGINAL's design, and compare the two rendered side by side BEFORE building. I skipped that for batch 2 (only read the schemas).** Tool built: `scratchpad/render_pack_section.py` renders a pack SECTION from `.work/master-packs/sections/*.liquid` with its own default settings (python-liquid + stubs: `{% style %}` -> `<style>`, schema stripped, font/image objects stubbed) into headless Edge; it works for `dtj-testimonials-slider`; reuse it for the remaining ports (NOT yet in the repo: copy to `tools/` if it is needed again). **Overflow root cause:** a long unbroken word gives the block a huge min-content width, and in a grid/flex parent (`auto` column, `align-items:flex-start`) the block stretched far past the container. New dev tool `tools/preview_overflow.py` (DTJ_APP_ROOT for app 2): renders every block with a 70-letter word in every text setting inside three hostile parents (grid auto, flex row, flex column) and reports px sticking out. BEFORE: all 9 section blocks overflowed (322 to 1,983 px). Fix in ONE place, the shared base class in `tools/build_blocks.py` BASE_CSS: `.dtj-b { min-width: 0; max-width: 100%; overflow-wrap: anywhere; }`. AFTER: all 9 section blocks AND all 7 DTJ Blocks blocks stay inside in all three layouts. This changes the generated CSS of BOTH apps, so BOTH need a deploy. **Slider redesign (matches the pack section's structure and numbers; copy/colours come from the merchant only):** centred header (heading 40px, rating strip, note), Trustpilot-style star TILES (coloured square + white star, tile colour a setting), cards 32/40px padding, radius 16, soft glow (setting), photo + stars + title + text cut after N words (default 20) with a "View more/less" toggle + author, round 50px arrows overlapping the cards (desktop only), optional dots, "View All"-style button (radius 8, colour setting), 3 cards desktop / 1.2 on phones (peek). Rating strip fields are TYPED by the merchant (word, score, stars count, note): nothing is calculated or invented. 61 settings; DTJ Sections now 92.1 KB of 100 KB (about half a block left). Tests: app 2 = 66, app 1 = 127, store builder OK; library rules ok; Theme Check 0 errors (advisory counts). Browser-verified desktop + phone against the pack render. **DEPLOYED (Harsh approved both exact commands): DTJ Sections `dtj-sections-4` (`https://dev.shopify.com/dashboard/141167028/apps/432103260161/versions/1156629987329`) and DTJ Blocks `dtj-blocks-22` (`https://dev.shopify.com/dashboard/141167028/apps/431619997697/versions/1156630446081`); Shopify refused nothing. NOT verified in the real theme: Harsh's next screenshot of the slider, and that the overflow is gone there.** (Commits `b778709`..`35fc035` predate this work; it is UNCOMMITTED.) Tests: 62 for this app (generic + `tests/test_sections.py`), library rules ok, Theme Check 0 errors / 6 advisory settings-count notes; app 1 still 127 tests. **Browser-verified (render, not Horizon)** phone and desktop; bugs found by looking and fixed: the CTA button took the browser/theme LINK colour (blue) as its fill, now `color: inherit` in its own CSS rule (a `color` and a `currentColor` fill in ONE rule trips library rule 19, so they are split and commented); slider showed no arrows/dots for 4 cards at 3 per view (page count rounded down: now ceil); Science Stats portrait image filled a whole phone screen (4:3 on phones); Root Science features were vertically centred leaving a gap (now top-aligned). Mutation lessons: Liquid has no `(a, b, c)` list literal for loops (caught before building); `and`/`or` mixing evaluates right to left (rewritten as nested ifs); test counts must use exact class strings (`class="dtj-rs__feature"`, not the prefix). **Next:** Harsh approves the deploy (`shopify app deploy --path "services\block-library-sections" ...`), then adds the five new blocks in the editor and screenshots them. ### 0.3 Background
- **What this is:** an automation system for **Digital Tushar Joshi (DTJ)**, a performance-marketing agency in India run with **Harsh** (developer, the person you work for). The long-term goal is to run the repeatable parts of the agency (audits, store builds, reporting, monitoring, ad guard) with little manual work, **without risking client money, accounts or data**. Plan: `RUNBOOK.md`. Rules: `CLAUDE.md`.
- **What the last sessions built (5 Oct 2026, one very long session):**
  1. A **Shopify store-provisioning service** (`services/store-builder/provision/`) that takes a brand config + product sheet + images and puts a theme, draft products and template settings on a store, with safety rules in code. 88 tests.
  2. A **proprietary block library delivered as a Shopify theme app extension** (`services/block-library/`): app "DTJ Blocks", with a **licence kill switch** (a flag we control that makes the blocks disappear). 7 blocks live; 120 tests as of 6 Oct 2026 (was 104).
- **Where it runs:** all work so far is on **Harsh's own test store** `elevate-with-harsh.myshopify.com` (type "Client Transfer", plan Development). **No real client store has been touched.** The live theme on that store was never touched.
- **State right now:** the active release is app version **`dtj-blocks-13`** (released 6 Oct 2026 by Harsh from his PowerShell; 13 = reels sound button + editor notes for empty blocks). The product-list fix (`b8ea0ad`) is built and tested but NOT released (section 0.2, step 1). What is **NOT verified** is how any block *looks* (the assistant has no browser; Harsh checks in the theme editor) and the **add-to-cart behaviour of the Add-ons block** in the real theme.
- **Git (6 Oct 2026):** this folder IS a git repo, remote = private `https://github.com/harsh-shopify-dev/dtj-agency-automation`. **GitHub has only `07ab47a` and `4a27d34`** on branch `chore/initial-import` (its default branch; no `main`, no branch protection yet: Harsh to fix in GitHub settings, item G). **Everything after that is LOCAL ONLY on this machine**: `chore/initial-import` is 2 commits ahead (`6a65115`, `286cd3f`), and the working branch **`feature/reels-sound-button`** builds on it (`be4a415` reels sound, `94af4c2` empty-block notes = the source of the released `dtj-blocks-13`, `b8ea0ad` product-list fix, plus HANDOFF commits). Pushing it (backup + pull request) needs Harsh's OK.
- **Shopify CLI access from the assistant's shell:** works only for `shopify store execute` (stored store token: read-only queries on elevate-with-harsh worked). Theme commands and app deploys did NOT work in the desktop-app session (section 0.1); in a terminal session they worked on 5 Oct. Check with the read-only `shopify theme list --store elevate-with-harsh.myshopify.com` before relying on it. Never run `shopify auth logout`, never read the CLI's login file.
- **Never do without Harsh's explicit approval of that exact action:** push/publish a theme, create or change products, deploy/release the app, flip the licence flag, send any message. Ask with the exact command and what it changes (section 2).
